It started right around the time my aunt came to live with us.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do over the past four months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to look forward to doing my watercolors, going to my dance class, and taking walks with the ladies in the neighborhood. But lately... I just leave my paints sitting there. Like I was just saying... I really just can't make myself care anymore. Even if I were to force myself to sit down with my watercolors, I don't get any joy out of it. My clothes still fit about the same as they always have, so... no, I don't think my weight has gone up or down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I have. My aunt lives with us, and sometimes I just get so tired or annoyed with her. But then... I start thinking I'm a terrible woman for feeling that way. I just feel like I'm bringing shame on my whole family because I can't be more grateful for what I have. It just makes me feel awful about myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having these thoughts about not waking up every day, or are there some days when you don't think about it at all?\n\nPatient: It's... well, it's pretty much every day lately. And... well, at home I just get stuck and fall behind on things, so my husband has had to take over doing most of the errands. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well... I guess what scared me enough to actually come in today is that I just keep thinking it would be a relief if I didn't wake up in the morning. I mean, I haven't tried to hurt myself or do anything like that. But just having those thoughts... it really frightened me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been going on for about four months now. It started right around the time my aunt came to live with us.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do over the past four months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to look forward to doing my watercolors, going to my dance class, and taking walks with the ladies in the neighborhood. But lately... I just leave my paints sitting there. I can't even make myself care enough to go out and do any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you're doing, even if you're not doing as many of them as you used to?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was just saying... I really just can't make myself care anymore. Even if I were to force myself to sit down with my watercolors, I don't get any joy out of it. It's why I just leave my things sitting there now. That good feeling I used to get from painting, or my dance class, or being out with the ladies... it's just completely gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Well, my husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. I guess I've noticed it too... even when I go to the market, I just stand there for too long before I pick up what I came for. I've completely stopped going to my dance class and my walking group. I also usually help out at the food pantry, but I've been missing my shifts there, too. So, yes, it's interfering quite a bit.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts and feelings have been present most of the day, nearly every day, over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes, most of the day, nearly every day. It's been going on for about four months now... ever since my aunt came to live with us. That's when it all started.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts and feelings have caused you significant distress or impairment in your social, occupational, or other areas of functioning?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's really getting in the way of my normal life. I've completely stopped going to my dance class and the walking group, and I'm missing my shifts at the food pantry. And... well, at home I just get stuck and fall behind on things, so my husband has had to take over doing most of the errands. So, yes, it's definitely causing a lot of problems for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of not wanting to wake up and your lack of enjoyment in activities are extremely severe, or more moderate?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say they're extremely severe, no. Maybe more mild to moderate. It's just... a constant heavy feeling, but it's not the worst it could possibly be, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: No, not restless. Actually, it's more the opposite. My husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. Even when I do manage to go to the market, I just... I stand there too long before picking up what I came for. It's like I'm moving in slow motion.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to really look forward to painting with my watercolors, and like I said, the dance class and walking with the ladies... but lately, I just leave my paints sitting out. I can't even make myself pick up the brush. I just... I can't make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of sadness or depression are present most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'd say so. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy sadness right in my chest. It's just... it's like I've already failed the day before it even starts. And that feeling pretty much stays with me until I go back to sleep.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Maybe more mild to moderate. It's just... a constant heavy feeling, but it's not the worst it could possibly be, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: No, not restless. Actually, it's more the opposite. My husband says I move like everything takes extra effort now. Even when I do manage to go to the market, I just...
